Our men in the trenches responded heavily, and word was sent back, not only to me, but to the commanders in the rear of the regiments along our line, that the Spaniards were attacking. It was imperative to see what was really going on, so I ran up to the trenches and looked out. At night it was far easier to place the Spanish lines than by day, because the flame-spurts shone in the darkness. I could soon tell that there were bodies of Spanish pickets or skirmishers in the jungle-covered valley, between their lines and ours, but that the bulk of the fire came from their trenches and showed not the slightest symptom of advancing; moreover, as is generally the case at night, the fire was almost all high, passing well overhead, with an occasional bullet near by.

I came to the conclusion that there was no use in our firing back under such circumstances; and I could tell that the same conclusion had been reached by Captain Ayres of the Tenth Cavalry on the right of my line, for even above the cracking of the carbines rose the Captain’s voice as with varied and picturesque language he bade his black troopers cease firing. The Captain was as absolutely fearless as a man can be. He had command of his regimental trenches that night, and, having run up at the first alarm, had speedily satisfied himself that no particular purpose was served by blazing away in the dark, when the enormous majority of the Spaniards were simply shooting at random from their own trenches, and, if they ever had thought of advancing, had certainly given up the idea. His troopers were devoted to him, would follow him anywhere, and would do anything he said; but when men get firing at night it is rather difficult to stop them, especially when the fire of the enemy in front continues unabated. When he first reached the trenches it was impossible to say whether or not there was an actual night attack impending, and he had been instructing his men, as I instructed mine, to fire low, cutting the grass in front. As soon as he became convinced that there was no night attack, he ran up and down the line adjuring and commanding the troopers to cease shooting, with words and phrases which were doubtless not wholly unlike those which the Old Guard really did use at Waterloo. As I ran down my own line, I could see him coming up his, and he saved me all trouble in stopping the fire at the right, where the lines met, for my men there all dropped everything to listen to him and cheer and laugh. Soon we got the troopers in hand, and made them cease firing; then, after awhile, the Spanish fire died down. At the time we spoke of this as a night attack by the Spaniards, but it really was not an attack at all. Ever after my men had a great regard for Ayres, and would have followed him anywhere. I shall never forget the way in which he scolded his huge, devoted black troopers, generally ending with “I’m ashamed of you, ashamed of you! I wouldn’t have believed it! Firing; when I told you to stop! I’m ashamed of you!”

===Chinese===
正面这些神枪手干的是完全正当的行当，他们的勇气和枪法理应赢得一切赞誉。我们后方的游击兵可就两样了。圣胡安之战时，这些人有不少就安置在树上。他们用的不是毛瑟枪，而是雷明顿枪（Remington），发射无烟火药和包铜子弹。在水壶山上击中我身旁温斯洛·克拉克（Winslow Clark）的，正是这样一发子弹；论远距离交战，雷明顿枪当然远不如毛瑟枪，可用于近距离的丛林活计却同样顶用，因为它们也用无烟火药。我们的部队推进、战壕里的西班牙人和山后的预备队溃逃之后，树上的游击兵来不及脱身，结果就被撇在了我军防线之后。我们从俘获的俘虏口中得知，西班牙军官一直刻意往士兵脑子里灌输一种信念：美国人是绝不留俘的；我想正因如此，这些游击兵才不肯投降；因为我们后来发现，西班牙人一旦确信我们会善待他们，是巴不得投降的。无论如何，这些游击兵守在他们的树上不停手，亮出来的不只是勇气，还有恣意的残忍与野蛮。他们有时也朝成队的武装人员开枪，可他们更中意的猎物，是那些手无寸铁的勤杂人员——医生、随军牧师、医护兵。他们朝用担架抬伤员的人开枪，朝开到前方来的医生开枪，朝着手举行安葬仪式的随军牧师开枪；所有这些非战斗人员臂上醒目的红十字袖标，非但护不住他们，反倒似乎使他们成了游击枪火特意关照的靶子。他们闹得实在不像话，当天下午和次日早晨，我派出精选的神枪手去搜猎他们，挑的自然既是头等的林地好手、山里人，又兼枪法出众。我的神枪手们对这些游击兵恨之入骨，对他们毫不留情。他们有条不紊地展开搜猎，在游击兵自己的这门营生上显出远胜一筹的手段，打死了十一个，我的人却连一根毫毛都没伤着。在这桩差事里立下显著战功的两个人，是古德温（Goodwin）和普罗菲特（Proffit）两名士兵，都在亚利桑那的连里，不过一个生在加利福尼亚，一个生在北卡罗来纳。古德温是天生的射手，不但步枪、左轮枪弹无虚发，投石索也一样在行。普罗菲特尽可以充作约翰·福克斯与默弗里小姐（Miss Murfree）笔下山地人的典型。他身量高挑，筋骨结实，相貌堂堂，膂力惊人，枪法出众，而且是彻头彻尾的好兵。他父亲是邦联军军官，从行伍一步步升上去的；倘若战争打得再久些，儿子也会同样往上升。话虽如此，倘若委任状在我手里，我很乐意给他一个，正如团里另外许多人我也乐意给一样。普罗菲特是个阴沉寡言的人，后来染上热病，病得不轻；作为对其良好军人操行的奖赏，他常获破格的优待；可他对热病和优待一概以同样的铁一般的冷淡处之，从不抱怨，也从不表露满意。

天黑前，神枪手们回来了。不久，我把警戒哨和前哨远远设到前方丛林里，杜绝一切遭袭的可能。入夜之后，远远的右侧山口上忽然窜起一堆堆火。火齐齐升起，我们怎么也参不透。商量了半天，我们断定那必定是城外来援的部队打给圣地亚哥城里西班牙人的信号——因为我们还不知道增援早已进城；古巴人全然无力阻止西班牙正规军想开到哪里就开到哪里。正当我们对这些篝火苦苦琢磨、把它归之于西班牙人的某种阴谋时，看样子同样纳闷的西班牙人，却把它算作起义军同我军联络的尝试。于是双方都警觉起来，西班牙人想必加强了我前方丛林里的外围小队，因为他们突然袭击了我们的一个警戒哨，把克罗克特（Crockett）重伤击倒。他由其余士兵抬了回来。显然西班牙防线有点紧张，因为这阵爆豆般的射击过后，紧跟着便是他们战壕和炮兵阵地重炮与步枪的一场猛烈火力。我们战壕里的人也重重还击，消息一路传回——不光传到我这里，也传到沿我线各团后方的指挥官那里——说西班牙人进攻了。必须看清究竟，我便跑上战壕向外张望。夜里辨认西班牙防线比白天容易得多，因为一团团火舌在黑暗中发亮。我很快看出，他们防线与我们防线之间那片丛林覆盖的山谷里，有成股的西班牙警戒哨或散兵，但大部分火力来自他们的战壕，丝毫没有推进的迹象；况且，同夜里的一般情形一样，枪口几乎全抬得老高，子弹从头顶上老远飞过，偶尔才有一颗从近旁掠过。

我得出结论：在这样的情形下还击毫无用处；而我也听得出，我防线右翼第十骑兵团的艾尔斯上尉（Captain Ayres）也得出了同样的结论，因为卡宾枪的爆响都压不住他的嗓门——他正用五花八门、绘影绘声的言辞呵令他的黑人士兵停止射击。这位上尉的无所畏惧，到了人所可能的极致。那晚由他掌管本团战壕，一有警报他就赶了上来，很快就确信：朝黑地里瞎放枪办不成任何事——西班牙人绝大多数不过是缩在自己战壕里乱放，倘若起过推进的念头，也肯定早就打消了。他的士兵爱戴他，跟他到哪里都愿意，他叫干什么就干什么；可是士兵们夜里一旦开了枪就很难叫停，尤其当前面敌人的火力仍不减时。他刚到战壕时，还说不准是否真有夜袭迫在眉睫，他也曾像我嘱咐我的人那样嘱咐士兵往低处打，扫跟前的草。一旦确信没有夜袭，他就沿着防线跑上跑下，连劝带命士兵停火，那些词句想必同老近卫军在滑铁卢真用过的相去不远。我沿着自己的防线跑下去时，望见他正从他的防线上跑上来；两线相接的右端，他替我省去了停火的全部麻烦，因为我那边的人全都撂下手里的东西去听他骂，为他欢呼发笑。我们很快把士兵管束住，叫他们停了火；过了一阵，西班牙人的火力也低落下去。当时我们把这件事说成西班牙人的夜袭，其实根本算不上什么袭击。从那以后，我的人对艾尔斯敬重有加，愿意跟他到任何地方。他训斥他那高大而赤诚的黑人士兵的样子，我永远不会忘记，结尾总是那句：「我替你们害臊，替你们害臊！我简直不敢相信！叫你们停了还放！我替你们害臊！」

===Original===
That night we spent in perfecting the trenches and arranging entrances to them, doing about as much work as we had the preceding night. Greenway and Goodrich, from their energy, eagerness to do every duty, and great physical strength, were peculiarly useful in this work; as, indeed, they were in all work. They had been up practically the entire preceding night, but they were too good men for me to spare them, nor did they wish to be spared; and I kept them up all this night too. Goodrich had also been on guard as officer of the day the night we were at El Poso, so that it turned out that he spent nearly four days and three nights with practically hardly any sleep at all.

Next morning, at daybreak, the firing began again. This day, the 3rd, we suffered nothing, save having one man wounded by a sharpshooter, and, thanks to the approaches to the trenches, we were able to relieve the guards without any difficulty. The Spanish sharpshooters in the trees and jungle nearby, however, annoyed us very much, and I made preparations to fix them next day. With this end in view I chose out some twenty first-class men, in many instances the same that I had sent after the guerillas, and arranged that each should take his canteen and a little food. They were to slip into the jungle between us and the Spanish lines before dawn next morning, and there to spend the day, getting as close to the Spanish lines as possible, moving about with great stealth, and picking off any hostile sharpshooter, as well as any soldier who exposed himself in the trenches. I had plenty of men who possessed a training in woodcraft that fitted them for this work; and as soon as the rumor got abroad what I was planning, volunteers thronged to me. Daniels and Love were two of the men always to the front in any enterprise of this nature; so were Wadsworth, the two Bulls, Fortescue, and Cowdin. But I could not begin to name all the troopers who so eagerly craved the chance to win honor out of hazard and danger.

Among them was good, solemn Fred Herrig, the Alsatian. I knew Fred’s patience and skill as a hunter from the trips we had taken together after deer and mountain sheep through the Bad Lands of the Little Missouri. He still spoke English with what might be called Alsatian variations﻿—he always spoke of the gun detail as the “gondetle,” with the accent on the first syllable﻿—and he expressed a wish to be allowed “a holiday from the gondetle to go after dem gorrillas.” I told him he could have the holiday, but to his great disappointment the truce came first, and then Fred asked that, inasmuch as the “gorrillas” were now forbidden game, he might be allowed to go after guinea-hens instead.

Even after the truce, however, some of my sharpshooters had occupation, for two guerillas in our rear took occasional shots at the men who were bathing in a pond, until one of our men spied them, when they were both speedily brought down. One of my riflemen who did best at this kind of work, by the way, got into trouble because of it. He was much inflated by my commendation of him, and when he went back to his troop he declined to obey the first Sergeant’s orders on the ground that he was “the Colonel’s sharpshooter.” The Lieutenant in command, being somewhat puzzled, brought him to me, and I had to explain that if the offence, disobedience of orders in face of the enemy, was repeated he might incur the death penalty; whereat he looked very crestfallen. That afternoon he got permission, like Fred Herrig, to go after guinea-hens, which were found wild in some numbers round about; and he sent me the only one he got as a peace offering. The few guinea-hens thus procured were all used for the sick.

===Chinese===
这一夜我们用来完善战壕、安排进出口，干的活计同前一夜不相上下。格林韦和古德里奇凭着他们的干劲、事事争先的热忱和过人的体力，在这桩工程里尤其得力；其实干什么活他们都是这样。前一夜他们几乎整宿没睡，但他们太出色了，我舍不得让他们歇，他们自己也不愿歇；这一夜我又让他们熬了个通宵。在埃尔波索（El Poso）的那晚，古德里奇还当值日官执勤过，结果算下来，他几乎四天三夜没怎么合过眼。

次日清晨，天一亮枪声又起。这一天，即 3 日，我们未受什么损失，只有一人被神枪手打伤；多亏有了进战壕的交通壕，换岗毫无困难。不过，附近树上和丛林里的西班牙神枪手搅得我们不堪其烦，我便着手准备第二天收拾他们。为达此目的，我挑了二十来个一流好手——多半就是先前派去搜猎游击兵的那批人——吩咐每人带上水壶和一点吃食。他们要在次日天亮前溜进我军与西班牙防线之间的丛林，在那里待上一整天，尽可能贴近西班牙防线，潜踪蹑迹地游走，专拣敌方神枪手打，也打战壕里探头露面的士兵。我手下多的是受过林中技艺训练、堪当此任的人；风声刚一传开我在筹划什么，志愿者便蜂拥而来。丹尼尔斯（Daniels）和洛夫（Love）是每逢这类差事必打头阵的两个；沃兹沃思（Wadsworth）、布尔家两兄弟（the two Bulls）、福蒂斯丘（Fortescue）和考丁（Cowdin）也是。可是那些渴盼着从风险危难中博取荣誉的士兵，我根本无从一一列举。

其中就有那位厚道而一本正经的弗雷德·赫里希（Fred Herrig），阿尔萨斯人。弗雷德做猎人的耐心与本事，我是从我们结伴在小密苏里河（Little Missouri）的恶地里猎鹿和猎大角羊的旅行中领教过的。他讲英语仍带着不妨称之为阿尔萨斯腔的变化——他总把炮组（gun detail）说成「贡德特尔」（gondetle），重音落在头一个音节上——他表示希望准他「告一天假，别守着贡德特尔，去打那些猩猩（dem gorrillas）」。我告诉他假可以准，不料停战先来了，令他大失所望；于是弗雷德又提出，既然「猩猩」如今成了禁猎之物，可否准他改去打珠鸡（guinea-hen）。

不过即便停战之后，我的神枪手有的还有营生，因为后方有两个游击兵时不时朝在池塘里洗澡的士兵开枪，直到我们的一个人发现了他们，两个便都很快被击落。顺便说一句，我的一个步枪手在这类活计上干得最出色，却因此惹了麻烦。他挨了我一顿夸，颇为飘飘然，回到连里便抗命不遵一级军士的命令，理由是他乃是「上校的神枪手」。连里主事的中尉有点摸不着头脑，把他带到我这里，我不得不向他说明：他这桩罪名——临敌抗命——倘若再犯，是可能掉脑袋的；闻言他一脸的颓丧。当天下午，他像弗雷德·赫里希一样获准去打珠鸡——附近野生的珠鸡颇有几只；他把打到的唯一一只送来给我，算作赔罪的礼物。这样弄来的几只珠鸡，全数都给了病号。

===Original===
Dr. Church had established a little field hospital under the shoulder of the hill in our rear. He was himself very sick and had almost nothing in the way of medicine or supplies or apparatus of any kind, but the condition of the wounded in the big field hospitals in the rear was so horrible, from the lack of attendants as well as of medicines, that we kept all the men we possibly could at the front. Some of them had now begun to come down with fever. They were all very patient, but it was pitiful to see the sick and wounded soldiers lying on their blankets, if they had any, and if not then simply in the mud, with nothing to eat but hardtack and pork, which of course they could not touch when their fever got high, and with no chance to get more than the rudest attention. Among the very sick here was gallant Captain Llewellen. I feared he was going to die. We finally had to send him to one of the big hospitals in the rear. Doctors Brewer and Fuller of the Tenth had been unwearying in attending to the wounded, including many of those of my regiment.

At twelve o’clock we were notified to stop firing and a flag of truce was sent in to demand the surrender of the city. The negotiations gave us a breathing spell.

That afternoon I arranged to get our baggage up, sending back strong details of men to carry up their own goods, and, as usual, impressing into the service a kind of improvised pack-train consisting of the officers’ horses, of two or three captured Spanish cavalry horses, two or three mules which had been shot and abandoned and which our men had taken and cured, and two or three Cuban ponies. Hitherto we had simply been sleeping by the trenches or immediately in their rear, with nothing in the way of shelter and only one blanket to every three or four men. Fortunately there had been little rain. We now got up the shelter tents of the men and some flies for the hospital and for the officers; and my personal baggage appeared. I celebrated its advent by a thorough wash and shave.

===Chinese===
丘奇医生在我们后方山体的山肩下开设了一座小小的战地医院。他自己病得很重，药品、给养、器械几乎一样没有；可后方几座大战地医院里伤员的境况实在骇人——既缺看护，又缺药——所以我们把能留在前沿的士兵全留在了前沿。这时有些人已经开始发热病倒。他们个个都极能忍耐，可是眼看伤病士兵躺在毯子上——有毯子的还好，没有的就直接躺在泥里——除了硬面饼和咸猪肉别无吃的（发高烧时自然一口也沾不得），得到的照料又粗陋得无以复加，实在令人心酸。病得最重的人当中，有英勇的卢埃伦上尉（Captain Llewellen）。我怕他要不行了，最后只得把他送往后方一所大医院。第十骑兵团的布鲁尔医生与富勒医生（Doctors Brewer and Fuller）照护伤员不遗余力，其中有许多是我团的人。

十二点，我们奉命停火，一面休战旗送进城去，要求该城投降。谈判给了我们一个喘息的间隙。

当天下午，我安排把我们的行李运上来，派出大队人手回后方去扛各自的物件，并且照例拉来当差，凑成一支临时驮队——由军官们的坐骑、两三匹缴获的西班牙军马、两三匹中弹被弃而我方士兵捡回来医好的骡子，再加两三匹古巴小马组成。在这之前，我们一直就睡在战壕边上或紧贴其后，谈不上任何遮蔽，每三四个人才合一条毯子。所幸雨水不多。这时士兵们的宿营帐篷运了上来，还给医院和军官弄到几块天幕；我个人的行李也到了。为庆祝它的降临，我痛痛快快洗了个澡、刮了脸。

===Original===
Later, I twice snatched a few hours to go to the rear and visit such of my men as I could find in the hospitals. Their patience was extraordinary. Kenneth Robinson, a gallant young trooper, though himself severely (I supposed at the time mortally) wounded, was noteworthy for the way in which he tended those among the wounded who were even more helpless, and the cheery courage with which he kept up their spirits. Gievers, who was shot through the hips, rejoined us at the front in a fortnight. Captain Day was hardly longer away. Jack Hammer, who, with poor Race Smith, a gallant Texas lad who was mortally hurt beside me on the summit of the hill, had been on kitchen detail, was wounded and sent to the rear; he was ordered to go to the United States, but he heard that we were to assault Santiago, so he struggled out to rejoin us, and thereafter stayed at the front. Cosby, badly wounded, made his way down to the seacoast in three days, unassisted.

With all volunteer troops, and I am inclined to think with regulars, too, in time of trial, the best work can be got out of the men only if the officers endure the same hardships and face the same risks. In my regiment, as in the whole cavalry division, the proportion of loss in killed and wounded was considerably greater among the officers than among the troopers, and this was exactly as it should be. Moreover, when we got down to hard pan, we all, officers and men, fared exactly alike as regards both shelter and food. This prevented any grumbling. When the troopers saw that the officers had nothing but hardtack, there was not a man in the regiment who would not have been ashamed to grumble at faring no worse, and when all alike slept out in the open, in the rear of the trenches, and when the men always saw the field officers up at night, during the digging of the trenches, and going the rounds of the outposts, they would not tolerate, in any of their number, either complaint or shirking work. When things got easier I put up my tent and lived a little apart, for it is a mistake for an officer ever to grow too familiar with his men, no matter how good they are; and it is of course the greatest possible mistake to seek popularity either by showing weakness or by mollycoddling the men. They will never respect a commander who does not enforce discipline, who does not know his duty, and who is not willing both himself to encounter and to make them encounter every species of danger and hardship when necessary. The soldiers who do not feel this way are not worthy of the name and should be handled with iron severity until they become fighting men and not shams. In return the officer should carefully look after his men, should see that they are well fed and well sheltered, and that, no matter how much they may grumble, they keep the camp thoroughly policed.

After the cessation of the three days’ fighting we began to get our rations regularly and had plenty of hardtack and salt pork, and usually about half the ordinary amount of sugar and coffee. It was not a very good ration for the tropics, however, and was of very little use indeed to the sick and half-sick. On two or three occasions during the siege I got my improvised pack-train together and either took or sent it down to the seacoast for beans, canned tomatoes, and the like. We got these either from the transports which were still landing stores on the beach or from the Red Cross. If I did not go myself I sent some man who had shown that he was a driving, energetic, tactful fellow, who would somehow get what we wanted. Chaplain Brown developed great capacity in this line, and so did one of the troopers named Knoblauch, he who had dived after the rifles that had sunk off the pier at Daiquiri. The supplies of food we got in this way had a very beneficial effect, not only upon the men’s health, but upon their spirits. To the Red Cross and similar charitable organizations we owe a great deal. We also owed much to Colonel Weston of the Commissary Department, who always helped us and never let himself be hindered by red tape; thus he always let me violate the absurd regulation which forbade me, even in war time, to purchase food for my men from the stores, although letting me purchase for the officers. I, of course, paid no heed to the regulation when by violating it I could get beans, canned tomatoes, or tobacco. Sometimes I used my own money, sometimes what was given me by Woody Kane, or what was sent me by my brother-in-law, Douglas Robinson, or by the other Red Cross people in New York. My regiment did not fare very well; but I think it fared better than any other. Of course no one would have minded in the least such hardships as we endured had there been any need of enduring them; but there was none. System and sufficiency of transportation were all that were needed.

===Chinese===
后来我两次挤出几个钟头，到后方医院里探望能寻见的我团士兵。他们的忍耐力非同寻常。肯尼思·罗宾逊（Kenneth Robinson）是个英勇的年轻士兵，自己伤得很重（当时我以为他必死无疑），却因照料伤员中比他更无助的人的那份细心、以及鼓舞大家情绪的那份快活勇气而为人称道。基弗斯（Gievers）胯部被打穿，两个星期后就回到了前线。戴上尉（Captain Day）离开的时间也不比这长。杰克·哈默（Jack Hammer）——同他一起当厨房勤务的，还有可怜的雷斯·史密斯（Race Smith），那个得克萨斯的好小伙，在山顶上我身旁受了致命伤——负伤后被送往后方；上面命令他回美国去，可他听说我们要强攻圣地亚哥，便挣扎着赶回来归队，此后一直留在前线。科斯比（Cosby）伤势沉重，无人帮扶，三天里独自走到了海边。

对一切义勇部队——我想正规军在考验关头也是如此——唯有军官与士兵同甘共苦、同冒其险，才能让士兵拿出最好的表现。在我团，正如在整个骑兵师一样，军官伤亡的比例比士兵高出相当一截，而这正是理所应当。再说，真到了勒紧腰带的底子，无论住还是吃，官兵上下完全一个样。这就杜绝了一切抱怨。士兵们见军官们除了硬面饼也别无长物，团里便没有一个人好意思为同样清苦的伙食发牢骚；当大家一样露宿在战壕后方，当士兵们总是看见校级军官夜里在挖战壕时督工、巡哨查岗，他们便容不得自己队伍里有谁叫苦或偷懒。境况好转后，我支起自己的帐篷，稍稍分开来住，因为军官与士兵过分亲昵总归是错，无论士兵多么优秀；而靠示弱或娇惯士兵去博人缘，更当然是错中之错。士兵们绝不会敬重一个不执行纪律、不懂本职、必要时既不敢自己也不肯让他们去直面一切艰险的指挥官。不这么想的士兵不配军人之名，就该以铁的手腕严加管束，直到他们成为真正的战士而非冒牌货。作为回报，军官则应悉心照料自己的士兵，务必让他们吃饱住好，并且不管他们多么爱嘀咕，都得把营地保持得干干净净。

三天战斗停止后，口粮开始正常发放，硬面饼和咸猪肉很充足，糖和咖啡通常约有常量的一半。然而这口粮对热带而言算不得好，对病人和半病之人实在几乎无用。围城期间，有两三次我把那支临时驮队召集起来，亲自带着或派它到海边去弄豆子、番茄罐头之类。这些东西，我们或从仍在滩头卸货的运输船上弄到，或从红十字会（Red Cross）弄到。我自己不去时，就派个已证明有闯劲、有精力、有手腕的家伙去，他总会想方设法弄到我们要的东西。布朗随军牧师在这一行里显出了大本事，一个名叫克诺布劳赫（Knoblauch）的士兵也是——就是在代基里潜下码头外水底捞步枪的那位。这样弄来的食物给养，不仅大有益于士兵的健康，更有益于他们的士气。红十字会和类似的慈善团体，我们受惠良多。我们还大亏军需部的韦斯顿上校（Colonel Weston），他总是帮助我们，从不让文牍主义捆住手脚；比如那条荒唐规章——即使战时也禁止我为士兵从商店购买食物，却准我为军官购买——他就始终听任我违反。当然，只要违反它能弄到豆子、番茄罐头或烟草，我从不把那规章放在心上。有时我用自己的钱，有时用伍迪·凯恩（Woody Kane）给我的钱，或我妹夫道格拉斯·罗宾逊（Douglas Robinson）以及纽约其他红十字会人士寄来的钱。我团的伙食算不得好；但我想比别的团都好。当然，倘若这些苦有非受不可的必要，谁也不会对我们吃的这点苦有半句怨言；可事实上毫无必要。所需要的，不过是像样的运输体系和充足的运力而已。

===Original===
On one occasion a foreign military attaché visited my headquarters together with a foreign correspondent who had been through the Turco-Greek war. They were both most friendly critics, and as they knew I was aware of this, the correspondent finally ventured the remark, that he thought our soldiers fought even better than the Turks, but that on the whole our system of military administration seemed rather worse than that of the Greeks. As a nation we had prided ourselves on our business ability and adroitness in the arts of peace, while outsiders, at any rate, did not credit us with any especial warlike prowess; and it was curious that when war came we should have broken down precisely on the business and administrative side, while the fighting edge of the troops certainly left little to be desired.

I was very much touched by the devotion my men showed to me. After they had once become convinced that I would share their hardships, they made it a point that I should not suffer any hardships at all; and I really had an extremely easy time. Whether I had any food or not myself made no difference, as there were sure to be certain troopers, and, indeed, certain troop messes, on the lookout for me. If they had any beans they would send me over a cupful, or I would suddenly receive a present of doughnuts from some ex-roundup cook who had succeeded in obtaining a little flour and sugar, and if a man shot a guinea-hen it was all I could do to make him keep half of it for himself. Wright, the color sergeant, and Henry Bardshar, my orderly, always pitched and struck my tent and built me a bunk of bamboo poles, whenever we changed camp. So I personally endured very little discomfort; for, of course, no one minded the two or three days preceding or following each fight, when we all had to get along as best we could. Indeed, as long as we were under fire or in the immediate presence of the enemy, and I had plenty to do, there was nothing of which I could legitimately complain; and what I really did regard as hardships, my men did not object to﻿—for later on, when we had some leisure, I would have given much for complete solitude and some good books.

Whether there was a truce, or whether, as sometimes happened, we were notified that there was no truce but merely a further cessation of hostilities by tacit agreement, or whether the fight was on, we kept equally vigilant watch, especially at night. In the trenches every fourth man kept awake, the others sleeping beside or behind him on their rifles; and the Cossack posts and pickets were pushed out in advance beyond the edge of the jungle. At least once a night at some irregular hour I tried to visit every part of our line, especially if it was dark and rainy, although sometimes, when the lines were in charge of some officer like Wilcox or Kane, Greenway or Goodrich, I became lazy, took off my boots, and slept all night through. Sometimes at night I went not only along the lines of our own brigade, but of the brigades adjoining. It was a matter of pride, not only with me, but with all our men, that the lines occupied by the Rough Riders should be at least as vigilantly guarded as the lines of any regular regiment.

===Chinese===
有一回，一位外国武官同一名经历过希土战争（Turco-Greek war）的外国记者造访我的指挥部。两人都是最友好的批评者，他们也知道我明白这一点，于是那位记者终于斗胆说出看法：依他看，我们的士兵打起仗来比土耳其人还要强，但总的说来，我们的军事行政体制似乎比希腊人的还要糟。作为一个民族，我们素来以经商的本事与和平技艺上的精明自诩，而外人反正也不认为我们有什么特出的武功；奇怪的是，战争一来，我们恰恰垮在商务与行政这一面，而部队的战斗锋芒却实在无可挑剔。

士兵们待我的那份深情，令我很是感动。他们一旦确信我愿与他们同甘共苦，便认定我根本不该受任何苦；而我过的日子实在舒服极了。我自己有没有吃的根本无关紧要，因为总有那么几个士兵——甚至有那么几个连的伙食——在暗中照应我。他们有豆子，就要给我送一杯过来；哪位昔日赶拢畜群的厨子弄到了一点面粉和糖，我会突然收到一份炸面圈；谁打到了一只珠鸡，我得费好大的劲才能让他给自己留下一半。每逢移营，掌旗中士赖特（Wright）和我的勤务兵亨利·巴德沙尔（Henry Bardshar）总是替我搭帐篷、拆帐篷，用竹竿给我搭铺。所以我个人几乎没吃什么苦头；当然，每场战斗前后那两三天，大家一齐将就度日，谁也不会计较。的确，只要我们还在敌人炮火之下、还在敌人眼皮底下，只要我有的是事做，就没有一件是我有理由抱怨的；而我真正视为苦楚的那些事，我的士兵们倒不反对——因为后来我们有了些闲暇，我肯出大价钱去换的，不过是清静独处和几本好书罢了。

无论有没有停战，无论——有时是这样——是否接到通知说并无停战、只是双方默契地再度休战，也无论打不打仗，我们始终同样警戒，夜间尤其如此。战壕里每四人留一人醒着，其余的人抱着步枪睡在他身旁或身后；哥萨克式前哨和警戒哨则前推到丛林边缘之外。每夜至少一次，我总挑个不固定的时辰巡视全防线，天黑下雨时尤其如此；不过有时防线交给威尔科克斯（Wilcox）或凯恩、格林韦或古德里奇这样的军官掌管，我就懒了，脱下靴子睡个整夜。有时夜里我不光走本旅的防线，也走毗邻各旅的防线。莽骑兵所守的防线，至少要同任何正规团的防线一样戒备森严——这不光是我的自豪，也是全团上下的自豪。

===Original===
Sometimes at night, when I met other officers inspecting their lines, we would sit and talk over matters, and wonder what shape the outcome of the siege would take. We knew we would capture Santiago, but exactly how we would do it we could not tell. The failure to establish any depot for provisions on the fighting-line, where there was hardly ever more than twenty-four hours’ food ahead, made the risk very serious. If a hurricane had struck the transports, scattering them to the four winds, or if three days of heavy rain had completely broken up our communication, as they assuredly would have done, we would have been at starvation point on the front; and while, of course, we would have lived through it somehow and would have taken the city, it would only have been after very disagreeable experiences. As soon as I was able I accumulated for my own regiment about forty-eight hours’ hardtack and salt pork, which I kept so far as possible intact to provide against any emergency.

If the city could be taken without direct assault on the intrenchments and wire entanglements, we earnestly hoped it would be, for such an assault meant, as we knew by past experience, the loss of a quarter of the attacking regiments (and we were bound that the Rough Riders should be one of these attacking regiments, if the attack had to be made). There was, of course, nobody who would not rather have assaulted than have run the risk of failure; but we hoped the city would fall without need arising for us to suffer the great loss of life which a further assault would have entailed.

Naturally, the colonels and captains had nothing to say in the peace negotiations which dragged along for the week following the sending in the flag of truce. Each day we expected either to see the city surrender, or to be told to begin fighting again, and toward the end it grew so irksome that we would have welcomed even an assault in preference to further inaction. I used to discuss matters with the officers of my own regiment now and then, and with a few of the officers of the neighboring regiments with whom I had struck up a friendship﻿—Parker, Stevens, Beck, Ayres, Morton, and Boughton. I also saw a good deal of the excellent officers on the staffs of Generals Wheeler and Sumner, especially Colonel Dorst, Colonel Garlington, Captain Howze, Captain Steele, Lieutenant Andrews, and Captain Astor Chanler, who, like myself, was a volunteer. Chanler was an old friend and a fellow big-game hunter, who had done some good exploring work in Africa. I always wished I could have had him in my regiment. As for Dorst, he was peculiarly fitted to command a regiment. Although Howze and Andrews were not in my brigade, I saw a great deal of them, especially of Howze, who would have made a nearly ideal regimental commander. They were both natural cavalrymen and of most enterprising natures, ever desirous of pushing to the front and of taking the boldest course. The view Howze always took of every emergency (a view which found prompt expression in his actions when the opportunity offered) made me feel like an elderly conservative.

===Chinese===
有时夜里遇见巡查各自防线的其他军官，我们就坐下来谈谈局势，揣想围城的结局会是何等光景。我们知道圣地亚哥终将拿下，但究竟怎样拿，谁也说不上来。火线上没有建立任何粮站，存粮向来不足二十四小时之需，这使风险非同小可。倘若一场飓风袭来，把运输船刮得七零八落，或者倘若三天大雨把我们的交通彻底断送——那是必然而无疑的——我们前沿就得饿到断粮；当然，我们总会熬过去、总会拿下城池，但那必得先经历非常难堪的日子。所以一俟可能，我就为本团囤下约四十八小时的硬面饼和咸猪肉，尽量原封不动存着，以备不时之需。

倘若不必对堑壕和铁丝网发动正面强攻就能拿下此城，我们衷心希望事情正是如此；因为凭以往的经验，这样的强攻意味着攻坚各团损失四分之一（而倘若非攻不可，我们横下心也要让莽骑兵位列攻坚各团之中）。当然，谁都宁肯强攻，也不愿冒失败的风险；但我们仍希望此城会陷落而无须我们再付出一场强攻必然带来的惨重生命代价。

送出休战旗之后的那一个星期里，和平谈判拖拖拉拉，上校们和上尉们自然没有置喙的余地。我们天天指望的不是眼看此城投降，就是奉命重新开打；到末了这事变得如此磨人，哪怕来一场强攻我们也欢迎，总好过继续无所作为。我时常同本团的军官们商谈局势，也同邻团几位交上了朋友的军官商谈——帕克、史蒂文斯、贝克（Beck）、艾尔斯、莫顿（Morton）和博顿（Boughton）。惠勒将军和萨姆纳将军（Generals Wheeler and Sumner）幕僚中那些出色的军官，我也常见，尤其是多斯特上校、加林顿上校（Colonel Garlington）、豪兹上尉（Captain Howze）、斯蒂尔上尉（Captain Steele）、安德鲁斯中尉（Lieutenant Andrews），还有阿斯特·钱纳上尉（Captain Astor Chanler）——他同我一样是义勇军人。钱纳是我的老朋友，也是猎大猎物的同道，在非洲做过些出色的探险工作。我一直希望能把他要到我的团里。至于多斯特，他天生就是当团长的料。豪兹和安德鲁斯虽不在我旅，我也常见到他们，尤其常常见到豪兹——他当团长几乎理想到了家。两人都是天生的骑兵，性子最为进取，总想冲到最前面、走最大胆的路。豪兹对每一次紧急事态所持的态度（一有机会便立刻见诸行动），常让我自觉像个老成的保守派。

===Original===
The week of non-fighting was not all a period of truce; part of the time was passed under a kind of nondescript arrangement, when we were told not to attack ourselves, but to be ready at any moment to repulse an attack and to make preparations for meeting it. During these times I busied myself in putting our trenches into first-rate shape and in building bomb-proofs and traverses. One night I got a detail of sixty men from the First, Ninth, and Tenth, whose officers always helped us in every way, and with these, and with sixty of my own men, I dug a long, zigzag trench in advance of the salient of my line out to a knoll well in front, from which we could command the Spanish trenches and blockhouses immediately ahead of us. On this knoll we made a kind of bastion consisting of a deep, semicircular trench with sandbags arranged along the edge so as to constitute a wall with loopholes. Of course, when I came to dig this trench, I kept both Greenway and Goodrich supervising the work all night, and equally of course I got Parker and Stevens to help me. By employing as many men as we did we were able to get the work so far advanced as to provide against interruption before the moon rose, which was about midnight. Our pickets were thrown far out in the jungle, to keep back the Spanish pickets and prevent any interference with the diggers. The men seemed to think the work rather good fun than otherwise, the possibility of a brush with the Spaniards lending a zest that prevented its growing monotonous.

Parker had taken two of his Gatlings, removed the wheels, and mounted them in the trenches; also mounting the two automatic Colts where he deemed they could do best service. With the completion of the trenches, bomb-proofs, and traverses, and the mounting of these guns, the fortifications of the hill assumed quite a respectable character, and the Gatling men christened it Fort Roosevelt, by which name it afterward went.6

During the truce various military attaches and foreign officers came out to visit us. Two or three of the newspaper men, including Richard Harding Davis, Caspar Whitney, and John Fox, had already been out to see us, and had been in the trenches during the firing. Among the others were Captains Lee and Paget of the British army and navy, fine fellows, who really seemed to take as much pride in the feats of our men as if we had been bound together by the ties of a common nationality instead of the ties of race and speech kinship. Another English visitor was Sir Bryan Leighton, a thrice-welcome guest, for he most thoughtfully brought to me half a dozen little jars of devilled ham and potted fruit, which enabled me to summon various officers down to my tent and hold a feast. Count von Gotzen, and a Norwegian attaché, Gedde, very good fellows both, were also out. One day we were visited by a travelling Russian, Prince X., a large, blond man, smooth and impenetrable. I introduced him to one of the regular army officers, a capital fighter and excellent fellow, who, however, viewed foreign international politics from a strictly trans-Mississippi standpoint. He hailed the Russian with frank kindness and took him off to show him around the trenches, chatting volubly, and calling him “Prince,” much as Kentuckians call one another “Colonel.” As I returned I heard him remarking: “You see, Prince, the great result of this war is that it has united the two branches of the Anglo-Saxon people; and now that they are together they can whip the world, Prince! they can whip the world!”﻿—being evidently filled with the pleasing belief that the Russian would cordially sympathize with this view.

===Chinese===
不打仗的那个星期并非全是停战期；有一段时间是在一种不伦不类的安排下度过的：我们奉命不得自行进攻，但要时刻准备击退进攻，并做好迎击的准备。这些日子里，我忙着把我们的战壕修到一流水准，构筑防炮掩体和横墙。一天夜里，我从第一、第九和第十骑兵团要到六十名劳役——这几个团的军官总是全力帮我们——加上我自己的六十人，从我防线的突出部向前，挖出一条长长的之字形战壕，一直通到前方相当远处的一座圆丘，从那里可以控制紧邻我们前方的西班牙战壕和碉堡。在这座圆丘上，我们修了一座类似棱堡的东西：一道深深的半圆形战壕，边缘码起沙袋，构成一堵带射击孔的墙。当然，挖这条战壕时，我让格林韦和古德里奇通宵督工；同样当然的是，我又把帕克和史蒂文斯拉来帮忙。由于投入了这么多人手，我们得以赶在月亮升起之前——约莫半夜——把工程推进到足以应付干扰的程度。我们的警戒哨远远前出到丛林里，顶住西班牙人的警戒哨，不让他们骚扰挖壕的人。士兵们似乎觉得这活儿与其说辛苦，不如说有趣——同西班牙人干上一仗的可能，给这桩工程添了兴味，叫它不至于变得单调。

帕克把他那加特林中的两门卸下轮子，架进了战壕；两门柯尔特速射自动炮也架在他认为最能出力的地方。随着战壕、防炮掩体和横墙的完工，加上这些炮的架设，山上的工事颇具了一番堂堂气象，加特林炮手们给它命名为罗斯福堡（Fort Roosevelt），此后它便一直沿用这个名字。⁶

停战期间，各国武官和外籍军官纷纷前来看我们。两三位新闻界的人，包括理查德·哈丁·戴维斯、卡斯帕·惠特尼和小约翰·福克斯，此前已经来过，还赶上枪战进过战壕。来客中还有英国陆军的李上尉与海军的佩吉特上尉（Captains Lee and Paget），都是好汉子，他们对我军将士的功绩似乎真怀着与有荣焉的自豪，仿佛维系我们的不是血缘与语言亲谊的纽带，而是同一国籍的纽带。另一位英国来客是布莱恩·莱顿爵士（Sir Bryan Leighton），一位再欢迎不过的宾客，因为他体贴入微，给我带来了六小罐辣味火腿酱和糖渍水果，使我能把几位军官召到我的帐篷里大摆筵席。冯·戈岑伯爵（Count von Gotzen）和挪威武官格德（Gedde）也来了，两位都是极好的人。有一天，一位四处游历的俄国人来访——X 亲王（Prince X.），身材高大，金发碧眼，圆滑而深不可测。我把他介绍给一位正规军军官——顶呱呱的战士、极好的汉子，只是看待外国的国际政治，用的是严格的密西西比河以西的眼光。他坦率而亲热地向这位俄国人打招呼，带他去参观战壕，滔滔不绝地闲聊，一口一个「亲王」，很像肯塔基人彼此称「上校」。我回来时听见他正说道：「您瞧，亲王，这场战争最了不起的结果，是把盎格鲁-撒克逊人的两支联合到了一起；如今他们拧成了一股绳，就能打遍天下无敌手了，亲王！打遍天下无敌手！」——他显然满怀愉快地相信，这位俄国人会由衷赞同这一高见。

===Original===
The foreign attaches did not always get on well with our generals. The two English representatives never had any trouble, were heartily admired by everybody, and, indeed, were generally treated as if they were of our own number; and seemingly so regarded themselves. But this was not always true of the representatives from Continental Europe. One of the latter﻿—a very good fellow, by the way﻿—had not altogether approved of the way he was treated, and the climax came when he said goodbye to the General who had special charge of him. The General in question was not accustomed to nice ethnic distinctions, and grouped all of the representatives from Continental Europe under the comprehensive title of “Dutchmen.” When the attaché in question came to say farewell, the General responded with a bluff heartiness, in which perhaps the note of sincerity was more conspicuous than that of entire good breeding: “Well, goodbye; sorry you’re going; which are you anyhow﻿—the German or the Russian?”

Shortly after midday on the 10th fighting began again, but it soon became evident that the Spaniards did not have much heart in it. The American field artillery was now under the command of General Randolph, and he fought it effectively. A mortar battery had also been established, though with an utterly inadequate supply of ammunition, and this rendered some service. Almost the only Rough Riders who had a chance to do much firing were the men with the Colt automatic guns, and the twenty picked sharpshooters, who were placed in the newly dug little fort out at the extreme front. Parker had a splendid time with the Gatlings and the Colts. With these machine guns he completely silenced the battery in front of us. This battery had caused us a good deal of trouble at first, as we could not place it. It was immediately in front of the hospital, from which many Red Cross flags were flying, one of them floating just above this battery, from where we looked at it. In consequence, for some time, we did not know it was a hostile battery at all, as, like all the other Spanish batteries, it was using smokeless powder. It was only by the aid of powerful glasses that we finally discovered its real nature. The Gatlings and Colts then actually put it out of action, silencing the big guns and the two field-pieces. Furthermore, the machine guns and our sharpshooters together did good work in supplementing the effects of the dynamite gun; for when a shell from the latter struck near a Spanish trench, or a building in which there were Spanish troops, the shock was seemingly so great that the Spaniards almost always showed themselves, and gave our men a chance to do some execution.

As the evening of the 10th came on, the men began to make their coffee in sheltered places. By this time they knew how to take care of themselves so well that not a man was touched by the Spaniards during the second bombardment. While I was lying with the officers just outside one of the bomb-proofs I saw a New Mexican trooper named Morrison making his coffee under the protection of a traverse high up on the hill. Morrison was originally a Baptist preacher who had joined the regiment purely from a sense of duty, leaving his wife and children, and had shown himself to be an excellent soldier. He had evidently exactly calculated the danger zone, and found that by getting close to the traverse he could sit up erect and make ready his supper without being cramped. I watched him solemnly pounding the coffee with the butt end of his revolver, and then boiling the water and frying his bacon, just as if he had been in the lee of the roundup wagon somewhere out on the plains.

===Chinese===
外国武官同我们的将军们并非总能相处融洽。两位英国代表从未有过龃龉，人人由衷敬慕他们，事实上他们普遍被当作自己人看待，看来他们自己也作如是观。但欧洲大陆来的代表就不尽然了。其中一位——顺便说一句，是个极好的汉子——对自己所受的待遇不甚满意，事情在他向专门照管他的那位将军道别时到了头。这位将军不惯于细分什么民族，把欧洲大陆来的代表一概归在「荷兰人」（Dutchmen）这个包罗万象的名目之下。当这位武官前来话别时，将军以直率的热情作答，其中诚恳的调子或许比十足的教养更惹眼：「呃，再见；你走我真舍不得；话说回来，你到底是哪国人来着——德国人还是俄国人？」

10 日午后不久，战斗重开，但很快就看出西班牙人无心恋战。美国野战炮兵此刻归伦道夫将军（General Randolph）指挥，他打得颇有章法。还建立了一个臼炮连，弹药供应虽然远远不足，也算出了些力。几乎仅有的一点大打出手的机会，落在操纵柯尔特速射自动炮的莽骑兵和二十名精选神枪手身上——后者被安置在最前端新挖的小堡垒里。帕克同他的加特林和柯尔特耍得痛快极了。他凭这些机枪，把我们正面的炮兵连彻底打哑了。这个炮兵连起初给我们添了不少麻烦，因为我们定位不到它。它正好处在医院前方，医院上空飘着许多红十字旗，其中一面从我们望去正飘在这炮位上方。结果有很长一段时间，我们压根不知道它是敌方炮位，因为它同所有其他西班牙炮兵一样，用的是无烟火药。还是靠高倍望远镜，我们才终于识破它的真面目。于是加特林和柯尔特真的把它打瘫了，压制住它的大炮和两门野战炮。不但如此，机枪同我们的神枪手配合，在放大甘油炸药炮的战果方面也干得不错；因为后者一发炮弹落在西班牙战壕近旁、或驻有西班牙军队的建筑附近时，震动似乎大得很，西班牙人几乎总要探头露面，让我们的人得以杀伤一阵。

10 日傍晚来临，士兵们开始在有遮蔽的地方煮咖啡。这时他们已极善照料自己，第二次炮击期间竟无一人被西班牙人伤着。我同军官们正躺在一座防炮掩体外，看见一个名叫莫里森（Morrison）的新墨西哥士兵，在山上高处一道横墙的掩护下煮咖啡。莫里森原是浸信会传道人，纯粹出于责任感抛下妻儿来从军，事实证明是名出色的士兵。他显然把危险区计算得分毫不差，发现只要紧贴横墙，就能坐直身子、从容备晚饭而不必蜷缩。我看着他不紧不慢地用左轮枪托捣咖啡豆，然后烧水、煎咸肉，仿佛正置身大平原上某辆赶拢畜群大车的背风处。

===Original===
By noon of next day, the 11th, my regiment with one of the Gatlings was shifted over to the right to guard the Caney road. We did no fighting in our new position, for the last straggling shot had been fired by the time we got there. That evening there came up the worst storm we had had, and by midnight my tent blew over. I had for the first time in a fortnight undressed myself completely, and I felt fully punished for my love of luxury when I jumped out into the driving downpour of tropic rain, and groped blindly in the darkness for my clothes as they lay in the liquid mud. It was Kane’s night on guard, and I knew the wretched Woody would be out along the line and taking care of the pickets, no matter what the storm might be; and so I basely made my way to the kitchen tent, where good Holderman, the Cherokee, wrapped me in dry blankets, and put me to sleep on a table which he had just procured from an abandoned Spanish house.

On the 17th the city formally surrendered and our regiment, like the rest of the army, was drawn up on the trenches. When the American flag was hoisted the trumpets blared and the men cheered, and we knew that the fighting part of our work was over.

Shortly after we took our new position the First Illinois Volunteers came up on our right. The next day, as a result of the storm and of further rain, the rivers were up and the roads quagmires, so that hardly any food reached the front. My regiment was all right, as we had provided for just such an emergency; but the Illinois newcomers had of course not done so, and they were literally without anything to eat. They were fine fellows and we could not see them suffer. I furnished them some beans and coffee for the elder officers and two or three cases of hardtack for the men, and then mounted my horse and rode down to headquarters, half fording, half swimming the streams; and late in the evening I succeeded in getting half a mule-train of provisions for them.

===Chinese===
次日，即 11 日中午，我团带着一门加特林移防右翼，守卫卡内大道。我们在新位置没打仗，因为等我们到时，最后一发零落的枪弹也已打完。当晚刮起了我们到此为止最猛的一场暴风雨，到半夜我的帐篷被掀翻了。我半个月来头一回脱光衣服睡觉，等我跳进热带瓢泼大雨里、在黑暗中摸索浸在烂泥汤里的衣裳时，我为自己贪图安逸受到了十足的惩罚。那晚轮到凯恩值岗，我知道倒霉的伍迪无论风暴多大，都会巡在线上照应警戒哨；于是我卑怯地摸进厨房帐篷，好心的切罗基人霍尔德曼（Holderman）用干毯子把我裹住，安置我睡在一张他刚从一幢被弃西班牙房屋里弄来的桌子上。

17 日，该城正式投降，我团同全军一样，列队于战壕之上。星条旗升起时，军号齐鸣，士兵欢呼，我们知道，我们这摊工作里打仗的部分结束了。

我们刚到新位置不久，第一伊利诺伊义勇团（First Illinois Volunteers）开到了我右翼。次日，由于那场风暴加上后续的降雨，河水暴涨，道路成了一锅烂泥，几乎没有任何食物送到前线。我团无恙，因为这种紧急情况我们早有准备；可新来的伊利诺伊人自然没做过准备，他们当真到了粒米无存的地步。他们是好汉子，我们不能眼看着他们挨饿。我给他们年长的军官们送了些豆子和咖啡，给士兵送了两三箱硬面饼，然后翻身上马，驰往司令部，一路的溪流半涉半泅；傍晚很晚的时候，我终于为他们弄到了半骡队的口粮。

===Original===
On the morning of the 3rd the Spaniards had sent out of Santiago many thousands of women, children, and other noncombatants, most of them belonging to the poorer classes, but among them not a few of the best families. These wretched creatures took very little with them. They came through our lines and for the most part went to El Caney in our rear, where we had to feed them and protect them from the Cubans. As we had barely enough food for our own men the rations of the refugees were scanty indeed and their sufferings great. Long before the surrender they had begun to come to our lines to ask for provisions, and my men gave them a good deal out of their own scanty stores, until I had positively to forbid it and to insist that the refugees should go to headquarters; as, however hard and merciless it seemed, I was in duty bound to keep my own regiment at the highest pitch of fighting efficiency.

As soon as the surrender was assured the refugees came streaming back in an endless squalid procession down the Caney road to Santiago. My troopers, for all their roughness and their ferocity in fight, were rather tenderhearted than otherwise, and they helped the poor creatures, especially the women and children, in every way, giving them food and even carrying the children and the burdens borne by the women. I saw one man, Happy Jack, spend the entire day in walking to and fro for about a quarter of a mile on both sides of our lines along the road, carrying the bundles for a series of poor old women, or else carrying young children. Finally the doctor warned us that we must not touch the bundles of the refugees for fear of infection, as disease had broken out and was rife among them. Accordingly I had to put a stop to these acts of kindness on the part of my men; against which action Happy Jack respectfully but strongly protested upon the unexpected ground that “The Almighty would never let a man catch a disease while he was doing a good action.” I did not venture to take so advanced a theological stand.

===Chinese===
3 日早晨，西班牙人从圣地亚哥放出数以千计的妇女、儿童和其他非战斗人员，其中大多数属于最贫苦的阶层，但也不乏上等人家。这些可怜人随身带的东西极少。他们穿过我军防线，大部分去了我们后方的埃尔卡内（El Caney），我们不得不在那里给他们饭吃，并保护他们不受古巴人侵害。我们自己的士兵尚且勉强糊口，难民的那份口粮实在少得可怜，他们的苦难也就深重。早在投降之前很久，他们就开始到我们防线来讨口粮，我的士兵从自己本已拮据的存粮里拿出不少接济他们，直到我不得不严令禁止，坚持让难民到司令部去领；因为这事无论显得多么冷酷无情，我都有责任使我团保持战斗效能的顶点。

投降一经确定，难民便沿着卡内大道向圣地亚哥川流不息地涌回，汇成一支望不到头的褴褛长队。我的士兵们尽管粗豪，尽管打起仗来凶猛，心地却毋宁说是软的，他们千方百计帮助这些可怜人，尤其是妇女和儿童，给他们吃的，甚至替他们抱孩子、扛女人们肩上的负担。我看见一个诨名「快活杰克」（Happy Jack）的士兵，一整天在沿路我防线两侧约四分之一英里的地段里来回奔走，替一个接一个穷苦的老太婆扛包袱，要不然就抱着小孩子。最后医生警告我们，千万不要再碰难民的包袱，以免感染——疫病已经发作，在他们中间蔓延正凶。于是我只好制止士兵们的这些善举；对此「快活杰克」恭敬而强烈地提出抗议，理由出人意料：「全能的上帝绝不会让一个行善的人染上疾病。」这么先进的神学立场，我可不敢采取。

## I Raising the Regiment / 第一章 组建骑兵团

===Original===
During the year preceding the outbreak of the Spanish War I was Assistant Secretary of the Navy. While my party was in opposition, I had preached, with all the fervor and zeal I possessed, our duty to intervene in Cuba, and to take this opportunity of driving the Spaniard from the Western World. Now that my party had come to power, I felt it incumbent on me, by word and deed, to do all I could to secure the carrying out of the policy in which I so heartily believed; and from the beginning I had determined that, if a war came, somehow or other, I was going to the front.

Meanwhile, there was any amount of work at hand in getting ready the navy, and to this I devoted myself.

Naturally, when one is intensely interested in a certain cause, the tendency is to associate particularly with those who take the same view. A large number of my friends felt very differently from the way I felt, and looked upon the possibility of war with sincere horror. But I found plenty of sympathizers, especially in the navy, the army, and the Senate Committee on Foreign Affairs. Commodore Dewey, Captain Evans, Captain Brownson, Captain Davis﻿—with these and the various other naval officers on duty at Washington I used to hold long consultations, during which we went over and over, not only every question of naval administration, but specifically everything necessary to do in order to put the navy in trim to strike quick and hard if, as we believed would be the case, we went to war with Spain. Sending an ample quantity of ammunition to the Asiatic squadron and providing it with coal; getting the battleships and the armored cruisers on the Atlantic into one squadron, both to train them in manoeuvring together, and to have them ready to sail against either the Cuban or the Spanish coasts; gathering the torpedo-boats into a flotilla for practice; securing ample target exercise, so conducted as to raise the standard of our marksmanship; gathering in the small ships from European and South American waters; settling on the number and kind of craft needed as auxiliary cruisers﻿—every one of these points was threshed over in conversations with officers who were present in Washington, or in correspondence with officers who, like Captain Mahan, were absent.

===Chinese===
西班牙战争（美西战争）爆发前的这一年，我担任海军助理部长（Assistant Secretary of the Navy）。在我的政党处于在野地位时，我曾倾尽自己全部的热忱与激情，宣扬我们干预古巴事务、并借这个机会把西班牙人逐出西半球的责任。如今我的政党已经执政，我便觉得责无旁贷，应当以言以行，竭尽所能促成这项我衷心信奉的政策的落实；而且从一开始我就打定了主意：一旦战争来临，无论如何，我都要上前线。

与此同时，手头还有数不清的整备海军的工作，我便一心扑在了这上面。

很自然，一个人对某项事业怀有强烈兴趣时，总会特别乐于同见解相同的人交往。我的许多朋友想法与我大相径庭，一想到可能爆发战争便由衷地感到恐惧。但我发现同情者也大有人在，尤其是在海军、陆军和参议院外交委员会里。杜威海军准将（Commodore Dewey）、埃文斯海军上校、布朗森海军上校、戴维斯海军上校——我常同这几位以及派驻华盛顿的其他各级海军军官进行长谈，反复推敲的不仅有海军行政的各种问题，更有一旦如我们所料与西班牙开战、要使海军整备到能迅速而沉重地出击所需办理的一切事项：向亚洲分舰队运送充足的弹药并为其配煤；把大西洋上的战列舰与装甲巡洋舰编成一支分队，既训练它们协同机动，又使它们随时可以出航，进击古巴或西班牙海岸；把鱼雷艇集结成一支练习支队；安排充分的实弹射击演习，借以提高我们的枪法水准；把散在欧洲和南美水域的小舰召回；确定辅助巡洋舰所需舰只的数量与种类——这每一个问题，都在与驻华盛顿军官的面谈中、或在与马汉海军上校（Captain Mahan）等不在京军官的通信里被反复推究过。

===Original===
As for the Senators, of course Senator Lodge and I felt precisely alike; for to fight in such a cause and with such an enemy was merely to carry out the doctrines we had both of us preached for many years. Senator Davis, Senator Proctor, Senator Foraker, Senator Chandler, Senator Morgan, Senator Frye, and a number of others also took just the right ground; and I saw a great deal of them, as well as of many members of the House, particularly those from the West, where the feeling for war was strongest.

Naval officers came and went, and Senators were only in the city while the Senate was in session; but there was one friend who was steadily in Washington. This was an army surgeon, Dr. Leonard Wood. I only met him after I entered the navy department, but we soon found that we had kindred tastes and kindred principles. He had served in General Miles’s inconceivably harassing campaigns against the Apaches, where he had displayed such courage that he won that most coveted of distinctions﻿—the Medal of Honor; such extraordinary physical strength and endurance that he grew to be recognized as one of the two or three white men who could stand fatigue and hardship as well as an Apache; and such judgment that toward the close of the campaigns he was given, though a surgeon, the actual command of more than one expedition against the bands of renegade Indians. Like so many of the gallant fighters with whom it was later my good fortune to serve, he combined, in a very high degree, the qualities of entire manliness with entire uprightness and cleanliness of character. It was a pleasure to deal with a man of high ideals, who scorned everything mean and base, and who also possessed those robust and hardy qualities of body and mind, for the lack of which no merely negative virtue can ever atone. He was by nature a soldier of the highest type, and, like most natural soldiers, he was, of course, born with a keen longing for adventure; and, though an excellent doctor, what he really desired was the chance to lead men in some kind of hazard. To every possibility of such adventure he paid quick attention. For instance, he had a great desire to get me to go with him on an expedition into the Klondike in midwinter, at the time when it was thought that a relief party would have to be sent there to help the starving miners.

===Chinese===
至于参议员们，洛奇参议员（Senator Lodge）自然与我的想法如出一辙；因为为这样的事业、同这样的敌人交战，不过是把我们两人多年来鼓吹的主张付诸实行罢了。戴维斯参议员、普罗克特参议员、福拉克参议员、钱德勒参议员、摩根参议员、弗莱参议员，还有另外许多人，也都立场分明、恰如其分；我常同他们见面，也常同众议院的许多议员见面——尤其是西部来的议员，那里主战的情绪最为强烈。

海军军官来来去去，参议员们也只有参议院开会期间才留在城里；但有一位朋友始终在华盛顿，他就是军医伦纳德·伍德医生（Dr. Leonard Wood）。我进海军部之后才认识他，但我们很快便发现彼此志趣相投、原则一致。他曾在迈尔斯将军麾下参加讨伐阿帕奇人（Apaches）的那些令人难以想象的磨人战役，在那里表现出的勇气，为他赢得了人人艳羡的最高荣誉——荣誉勋章（Medal of Honor）；他体魄之强健、耐力之超群，使他渐渐被公认为仅有的那么两三个能像阿帕奇人一样吃苦耐劳的白人；他的判断力又是那样出色，以致到战役末期，尽管身为军医，他却被委以实际指挥权，率领一支以上的远征队去清剿叛离的印第安匪帮。与我后来有幸共事的许多勇武之士一样，他把十足的男人气概，与完全的正直和品行高洁，以极高的程度集于一身。同一位理想崇高、鄙夷一切卑鄙龌龊，又具备刚健强悍的身心素质的人打交道，是一件快事——缺了这种素质，任何仅仅是消极的美德都无从弥补。他天生就是最高意义上的军人，而且像大多数天生的军人一样，生来就怀着对冒险的强烈渴望；他虽然是一位出色的医生，真正想要的却是有机会率领部下去经历某种凶险。凡是这种冒险的可能，他无不闻风而动。比方说，他曾极力怂恿我和他一起在隆冬远征克朗代克（Klondike），那正是人们认为必须派一支救援队去救助挨饿的矿工的时候。

===Original===
In the summer he and I took long walks together through the beautiful broken country surrounding Washington. In winter we sometimes varied these walks by kicking a football in an empty lot, or, on the rare occasions when there was enough snow, by trying a couple of sets of skis or snow-skates, which had been sent me from Canada.

But always on our way out to and back from these walks and sport, there was one topic to which, in our talking, we returned, and that was the possible war with Spain. We both felt very strongly that such a war would be as righteous as it would be advantageous to the honor and the interests of the nation; and after the blowing up of the *Maine*, we felt that it was inevitable. We then at once began to try to see that we had our share in it. The President and my own chief, Secretary Long, were very firm against my going, but they said that if I was bent upon going they would help me. Wood was the medical adviser of both the President and the Secretary of War, and could count upon their friendship. So we started with the odds in our favor.

At first we had great difficulty in knowing exactly what to try for. We could go on the staff of any one of several Generals, but we much preferred to go in the line. Wood hoped he might get a commission in his native State of Massachusetts; but in Massachusetts, as in every other State, it proved there were ten men who wanted to go to the war for every chance to go. Then we thought we might get positions as field-officers under an old friend of mine, Colonel﻿—now General﻿—Francis V. Greene, of New York, the Colonel of the Seventy-first; but again there were no vacancies.

===Chinese===
夏天，他和我常常一起长途漫步，走过华盛顿周边那片冈峦起伏的美丽乡野。冬天，我们有时换换花样，到空地上踢足球；赶上难得积雪够厚的日子，就试用几副从加拿大寄给我的滑雪板和雪上冰鞋。

但无论往返途中还是运动之余，我们的话题总会回到同一件事上，那就是可能与西班牙爆发的战争。我们都深信，这场战争既有利于国家的荣誉与利益，又是正义之举；「缅因」号（Maine）被炸毁之后，我们更觉得它已不可避免。于是我们立刻着手争取让自己在这场战争里占上一席之地。总统和我的顶头上司、海军部长朗（Secretary Long）都坚决反对我去，但他们说，如果我执意要去，他们愿意帮我。伍德是总统和陆军部长两人的医务顾问，也深得他们的友谊。这样，我们一起步就占了上风。

起初，我们简直不知道该去谋求什么才好。我们可以到好几位将军的参谋部里任职，但我们更想带兵打仗。伍德原本希望能在他的故乡马萨诸塞州谋个军职；可是在马萨诸塞，正如在其他各州一样，事实证明每个出征的机会背后都排着十个想去的人。后来我们又想，或许能在我的一位老朋友——现任上校、后来升为将军的纽约人弗朗西斯·V·格林（Francis V. Greene），第七十一团团长——麾下谋个野战军官的职位；可是依旧没有空缺。

===Original===
Our doubts were resolved when Congress authorized the raising of three cavalry regiments from among the wild riders and riflemen of the Rockies and the Great Plains. During Wood’s service in the Southwest he had commanded not only regulars and Indian scouts, but also white frontiersmen. In the Northwest I had spent much of my time, for many years, either on my ranch or in long hunting trips, and had lived and worked for months together with the cowboy and the mountain hunter, faring in every way precisely as they did.

Secretary Alger offered me the command of one of these regiments. If I had taken it, being entirely inexperienced in military work, I should not have known how to get it equipped most rapidly, for I should have spent valuable weeks in learning its needs, with the result that I should have missed the Santiago campaign, and might not even have had the consolation prize of going to Puerto Rico. Fortunately, I was wise enough to tell the Secretary that while I believed I could learn to command the regiment in a month, that it was just this very month which I could not afford to spare, and that therefore I would be quite content to go as Lieutenant-Colonel, if he would make Wood Colonel.

This was entirely satisfactory to both the President and Secretary, and, accordingly, Wood and I were speedily commissioned as Colonel and Lieutenant-Colonel of the First United States Volunteer Cavalry. This was the official title of the regiment, but for some reason or other the public promptly christened us the “Rough Riders.” At first we fought against the use of the term, but to no purpose; and when finally the Generals of Division and Brigade began to write in formal communications about our regiment as the “Rough Riders,” we adopted the term ourselves.

===Chinese===
国会授权从落基山脉与大平原（Great Plains）的荒野骑手和神枪手中征募三个骑兵团，我们的疑虑顿时烟消云散。伍德在西南部服役期间，不仅指挥过正规军和印第安侦察兵，也指挥过白人边民。而我多年来在西北部度过的时光，不是在自己的牧场里，就是在长途狩猎途中，与牛仔和山地猎人一起生活、一起干活，一待就是几个月，吃穿住行同他们完全一个样。

阿尔杰部长（Secretary Alger）提出让我指挥其中一个团。倘若我接受了，以我对军事工作全然无经验的样子，我根本不知道怎样才能以最快的速度把这个团装备起来，因为我得先花掉宝贵的几个星期去摸清它需要什么，结果就会错过圣地亚哥战役（Santiago campaign），说不定连去波多黎各的安慰奖也捞不着。所幸我还算明智，对部长说：我自信能在一个月内学会指挥这个团，可偏偏就是这一个月我抽不出来；因此，只要让伍德当上校，我完全乐意以中校衔入伍。

总统和部长对此完全满意。于是，伍德和我很快被任命为美国第一义勇骑兵团（First United States Volunteer Cavalry）的上校和中校。这是该团的正式名称，但不知怎么，公众立刻给我们起了「莽骑兵」（Rough Riders）这个诨名。起初我们还抵制这个叫法，却毫无用处；等到师、旅的将军们开始在正式公文里也称我们团为「莽骑兵」时，我们自己也就采用了这个名称。

===Original===
The mustering-places for the regiment were appointed in New Mexico, Arizona, Oklahoma, and Indian Territory. The difficulty in organizing was not in selecting, but in rejecting men. Within a day or two after it was announced that we were to raise the regiment, we were literally deluged with applications from every quarter of the Union. Without the slightest trouble, so far as men went, we could have raised a brigade or even a division. The difficulty lay in arming, equipping, mounting, and disciplining the men we selected. Hundreds of regiments were being called into existence by the National Government, and each regiment was sure to have innumerable wants to be satisfied. To a man who knew the ground as Wood did, and who was entirely aware of our national unpreparedness, it was evident that the ordnance and quartermaster’s bureaus could not meet, for some time to come, one-tenth of the demands that would be made upon them; and it was all-important to get in first with our demands. Thanks to his knowledge of the situation and promptness, we immediately put in our requisitions for the articles indispensable for the equipment of the regiment; and then, by ceaseless worrying of excellent bureaucrats, who had no idea how to do things quickly or how to meet an emergency, we succeeded in getting our rifles, cartridges, revolvers, clothing, shelter-tents, and horse gear just in time to enable us to go on the Santiago expedition. Some of the State troops, who were already organized as National Guards, were, of course, ready, after a fashion, when the war broke out; but no other regiment which had our work to do was able to do it in anything like as quick time, and therefore no other volunteer regiment saw anything like the fighting which we did.

Wood thoroughly realized what the Ordnance Department failed to realize, namely, the inestimable advantage of smokeless powder; and, moreover, he was bent upon our having the weapons of the regulars, for this meant that we would be brigaded with them, and it was evident that they would do the bulk of the fighting if the war were short. Accordingly, by acting with the utmost vigor and promptness, he succeeded in getting our regiment armed with the Krag-Jorgensen carbine used by the regular cavalry.

===Chinese===
团队的集结地定在新墨西哥、亚利桑那、俄克拉何马和印第安领地（Indian Territory）。组建之难不在于选人，而在于辞退人。要组建这个团的消息公布后不过一两天，联邦各地的申请书便如洪水般涌来。单就兵员而论，我们毫不费力就能编起一个旅、甚至一个师。真正的困难，在于为我们选中的人配发武器、装备、马匹，并施以纪律训练。联邦政府正在组建数以百计的团队，每个团都必然有数不清的需求要满足。对一个像伍德那样深知内情、又完全清楚我们全国战备何等空虚的人来说，军械署和军需署在相当一段时间内连各方所提要求的十分之一都满足不了，这是一望而知的；因此，抢在头里递交我团申请便至关重要。多亏他洞悉局势、行事果断，我们立即提出了装备团队所不可或缺的各项物资的申请单；随后又对那班全然不知快速办事、不知应付紧急状态为何物的优秀官僚们软磨硬泡、纠缠不休，终于赶在圣地亚哥远征出发之前，把步枪、弹药、左轮手枪、被服、宿营帐篷和马具悉数领到了手。有些州部队原已编为国民警卫队（National Guard），开战时自然算得上准备就绪——勉强够格的那种就绪；但没有任何一个需要做我们这种准备工作的团，能以差不多的速度完成它，因此也没有任何一支义勇团队，打过我们那样多的仗。

军械署没有认识到无烟火药的无比优越性，伍德却认识得一清二楚；况且他一心要让我们装备正规军的武器，因为这意味着我们会同正规军混编成旅，而倘若战争速战速决，显然要由正规军承担大部分战斗。于是，凭着雷厉风行的干劲和果断，他成功地让全团装备上了正规骑兵使用的克拉格-约根森卡宾枪（Krag-Jorgensen carbine）。

===Original===
It was impossible to take any of the numerous companies which were proffered to us from the various States. The only organized bodies we were at liberty to accept were those from the four Territories. But owing to the fact that the number of men originally allotted to us, 780, was speedily raised to 1,000, we were given a chance to accept quite a number of eager volunteers who did not come from the Territories, but who possessed precisely the same temper that distinguished our Southwestern recruits, and whose presence materially benefited the regiment.

We drew recruits from Harvard, Yale, Princeton, and many another college; from clubs like the Somerset, of Boston, and Knickerbocker, of New York; and from among the men who belonged neither to club nor to college, but in whose veins the blood stirred with the same impulse which once sent the Vikings over sea. Four of the policemen who had served under me, while I was President of the New York Police Board, insisted on coming﻿—two of them to die, the other two to return unhurt after honorable and dangerous service. It seemed to me that almost every friend I had in every State had some one acquaintance who was bound to go with the Rough Riders, and for whom I had to make a place. Thomas Nelson Page, General Fitzhugh Lee, Congressman Odell, of New York, Senator Morgan; for each of these, and for many others, I eventually consented to accept some one or two recruits, of course only after a most rigid examination into their physical capacity, and after they had shown that they knew how to ride and shoot. I may add that in no case was I disappointed in the men thus taken.

===Chinese===
各州主动送来的众多连队，我们一个也无法接纳。我们有权接受的成建制部队，仅限于来自那四个领地的。但由于最初分配给我们的兵员名额 780 人很快增加到 1,000 人，我们便有机会收下相当一批并非来自领地、却恰具我们西南部新兵那种气质的热血志愿者；他们的加入，使全团实实在在地受益。

我们的新兵来自哈佛、耶鲁、普林斯顿和许多别的大学；来自波士顿的萨默塞特俱乐部、纽约的尼克博克俱乐部这样的俱乐部；也来自那些既不属于俱乐部也不属于大学、血管里的血液却怀着当年送维京人（Vikings）扬帆出海的那股冲动的人。我任纽约警察委员会主席时麾下有四名警察，执意要来——两人战死，另外两人在光荣而危险的服役之后安然无恙地归来。在我看来，我在各州的几乎每一位朋友，都有一两个非跟莽骑兵走不可的相识，要我为其安插位置。托马斯·纳尔逊·佩奇、菲茨休·李将军、纽约州议员奥德尔、摩根参议员——为了这些人以及另外许多人，我最终都同意收下他们推荐的一两个新兵，当然，前提是对他们的体格做过最严格的检查，并确认他们会骑马、会打枪。我可以补充一句：这样收进来的人，没有一个让我失望过。

===Original===
Harvard being my own college, I had such a swarm of applications from it that I could not take one in ten. What particularly pleased me, not only in the Harvard but the Yale and Princeton men, and, indeed, in these recruits from the older States generally, was that they did not ask for commissions. With hardly an exception they entered upon their duties as troopers in the spirit which they held to the end, merely endeavoring to show that no work could be too hard, too disagreeable, or too dangerous for them to perform, and neither asking nor receiving any reward in the way of promotion or consideration. The Harvard contingent was practically raised by Guy Murchie, of Maine. He saw all the fighting and did his duty with the utmost gallantry, and then left the service as he had entered it, a trooper, entirely satisfied to have done his duty﻿—and no man did it better. So it was with Dudley Dean, perhaps the best quarterback who ever played on a Harvard Eleven; and so with Bob Wrenn, a quarterback whose feats rivalled those of Dean’s, and who, in addition, was the champion tennis player of America, and had, on two different years, saved this championship from going to an Englishman. So it was with Yale men like Waller, the high jumper, and Garrison and Girard; and with Princeton men like Devereux and Channing, the football players; with Larned, the tennis player; with Craig Wadsworth, the steeplechase rider; with Joe Stevens, the crack polo player; with Hamilton Fish, the ex-captain of the Columbia crew, and with scores of others whose names are quite as worthy of mention as any of those I have given. Indeed, they all sought entry into the ranks of the Rough Riders as eagerly as if it meant something widely different from hard work, rough fare, and the possibility of death; and the reason why they turned out to be such good soldiers lay largely in the fact that they were men who had thoroughly counted the cost before entering, and who went into the regiment because they believed that this offered their best chance for seeing hard and dangerous service. Mason Mitchell, of New York, who had been a chief of scouts in the Riel Rebellion, travelled all the way to San Antonio to enlist; and others came there from distances as great.

Some of them made appeals to me which I could not possibly resist. Woodbury Kane had been a close friend of mine at Harvard. During the eighteen years that had passed since my graduation I had seen very little of him, though, being always interested in sport, I occasionally met him on the hunting field, had seen him on the deck of the *Defender* when she vanquished the *Valkyrie*, and knew the part he had played on the *Navajoe*, when, in her most important race, that otherwise unlucky yacht vanquished her opponent, the Prince of Wales’s *Britannia*. When the war was on, Kane felt it his duty to fight for his country. He did not seek any position of distinction. All he desired was the chance to do whatever work he was put to do well, and to get to the front; and he enlisted as a trooper. When I went down to the camp at San Antonio he was on kitchen duty, and was cooking and washing dishes for one of the New Mexican troops; and he was doing it so well that I had no further doubt as to how he would get on.

===Chinese===
哈佛是我自己的母校，从那里来的申请书多得如蜂拥，以致我十中也取不了一。特别令我欣慰的是——不仅在哈佛人身上，在耶鲁人、普林斯顿人身上，而且一般说来在这批来自东部老牌各州的志愿者身上——他们都不谋求军官委任。他们几乎无一例外，以普通士兵的身份开始履职，并把这种精神保持到最后：只是一心想证明，再苦、再累、再险的差事他们也干得了；升迁和照顾，既不求，也不受。哈佛这批人实际上是由缅因州的盖伊·默奇（Guy Murchie）招募来的。他一场不落地打完了所有战斗，极为英勇地尽了职，然后以入伍时的身份——一名普通士兵——退伍，对尽过了本分心满意足——而尽本分尽得比他更好的，一个人也没有。达德利·迪安（Dudley Dean）如此，他也许是哈佛橄榄球校队有史以来最好的四分卫；鲍勃·雷恩（Bob Wrenn）也如此，这位四分卫的身手堪与迪安匹敌，而且还是美国的网球冠军，曾两度把这项冠军从英国人手里保住。耶鲁人中的跳高选手沃勒，还有加里森和吉拉德，也是如此；普林斯顿的橄榄球员德弗罗和钱宁、网球选手拉恩德、越野障碍赛骑手克雷格·沃兹沃思（Craig Wadsworth）、一流马球手乔·史蒂文斯（Joe Stevens）、哥伦比亚大学划船队前队长汉密尔顿·菲什（Hamilton Fish），以及几十个名字同样值得一提的人，莫不如此。的确，他们全都争先恐后地要挤进莽骑兵的行列，那股热切劲儿，仿佛等着他们的不是苦役、粗食和送命的可能，而是全然不同的美事；而他们后来之所以成为那样出色的士兵，很大程度上正因为这些人入伍之前早已把代价掂量得清清楚楚，之所以进这个团，是因为相信这是他们经受艰苦与危险服役的最好机会。纽约州的梅森·米切尔（Mason Mitchell）曾在里尔叛乱（Riel Rebellion）中当过侦察队长，千里迢迢赶到圣安东尼奥（San Antonio）入伍；从同样遥远的地方赶来投效的，也大有人在。

他们中有些人向我提出请求，叫我实在无法拒绝。伍德伯里·凯恩（Woodbury Kane）在哈佛时是我的密友。自我毕业以来，十八年过去，我难得见到他；不过由于我素来喜好运动，偶尔会在猎场上遇到他，曾在「防御者」号（Defender）战胜「瓦尔基里」号（Valkyrie）时见过他在甲板上的身影，也知道他在「纳瓦霍」号（Navajoe）上的那段经历——那条时运不济的帆船，正是在它最重要的一场比赛中，击败了对手、威尔士亲王的「不列颠尼亚」号（Britannia）。战争一来，凯恩觉得为国而战是自己义不容辞的事。他不谋求任何显赫职位，一心只想得到一个把分内之事做好并开上前线的机会，于是以普通士兵的身份入了伍。我到圣安东尼奥营地时，他正轮值伙房，在新墨西哥籍的一个连里烧菜洗碗；他干得那么出色，使我对他的前程再无半点疑虑。

===Original===
My friend of many hunts and ranch partner, Robert Munro Ferguson, of Scotland, who had been on Lord Aberdeen’s staff as a Lieutenant but a year before, likewise could not keep out of the regiment. He, too, appealed to me in terms which I could not withstand, and came in like Kane to do his full duty as a trooper, and like Kane to win his commission by the way he thus did his duty.

I felt many qualms at first in allowing men of this stamp to come in, for I could not be certain that they had counted the cost, and was afraid they would find it very hard to serve﻿—not for a few days, but for months﻿—in the ranks, while I, their former intimate associate, was a field-officer; but they insisted that they knew their minds, and the events showed that they did. We enlisted about fifty of them from Virginia, Maryland, and the Northeastern States, at Washington. Before allowing them to be sworn in, I gathered them together and explained that if they went in they must be prepared not merely to fight, but to perform the weary, monotonous labor incident to the ordinary routine of a soldier’s life; that they must be ready to face fever exactly as they were to face bullets; that they were to obey unquestioningly, and to do their duty as readily if called upon to garrison a fort as if sent to the front. I warned them that work that was merely irksome and disagreeable must be faced as readily as work that was dangerous, and that no complaint of any kind must be made; and I told them that they were entirely at liberty not to go, but that after they had once signed there could then be no backing out.

===Chinese===
我多次同去狩猎的老友、牧场合伙人、苏格兰人罗伯特·门罗·弗格森（Robert Munro Ferguson），一年之前还在阿伯丁勋爵（Lord Aberdeen）幕下当中尉，同样按捺不住要进这个团。他也用令我无法抗拒的言辞向我恳求，并且和凯恩一样，以普通士兵的身份进来尽他的全部职责，又和凯恩一样，凭着这样尽职责，为自己赢得了军官委任。

起初，对放这一类人入伍，我心里颇多踌躇，因为我无法确定他们是否真把代价掂量过，也担心他们会发现，当他们的旧日至交做着校官、他们却要在行伍间服役——不是几天，而是几个月——实在难以忍受；但他们坚称自己想清楚了，后来的事实也证明他们确实想清楚了。我们在华盛顿招收了约五十名这样的人，来自弗吉尼亚、马里兰和东北各州。在允许他们宣誓入伍之前，我把他们召集起来说明：一旦入伍，就必须准备不仅去打仗，还要去干士兵日常勤务中那种令人疲惫的单调苦工；必须准备像直面子弹一样直面热病；必须绝对服从，被派去守卫要塞要像被派上前沿一样痛快尽职。我警告他们，光是烦人讨厌的差事，也得像危险的差事一样痛快去干，而且不得有任何怨言；我还告诉他们，不去，完全听便，可一旦签字，就再没有反悔的余地。

===Original===
Not a man of them backed out; not one of them failed to do his whole duty.

These men formed but a small fraction of the whole. They went down to San Antonio, where the regiment was to gather and where Wood preceded me, while I spent a week in Washington hurrying up the different bureaus and telegraphing my various railroad friends, so as to insure our getting the carbines, saddles, and uniforms that we needed from the various armories and storehouses. Then I went down to San Antonio myself, where I found the men from New Mexico, Arizona, and Oklahoma already gathered, while those from Indian Territory came in soon after my arrival.

These were the men who made up the bulk of the regiment, and gave it its peculiar character. They came from the Four Territories which yet remained within the boundaries of the United States; that is, from the lands that have been most recently won over to white civilization, and in which the conditions of life are nearest those that obtained on the frontier when there still was a frontier. They were a splendid set of men, these Southwesterners﻿—tall and sinewy, with resolute, weather-beaten faces, and eyes that looked a man straight in the face without flinching. They included in their ranks men of every occupation; but the three types were those of the cowboy, the hunter, and the mining prospector﻿—the man who wandered hither and thither, killing game for a living, and spending his life in the quest for metal wealth.

===Chinese===
他们中没有一个人反悔退出，也没有一个人未尽到自己的全部职责。

这些人只占全团的一小部分。他们南下圣安东尼奥——全团将在那里集结，伍德先我而去；我则在华盛顿花了一个星期催促各署办事，给我那些铁路界的朋友们逐一拍电报，务必保证我们从各军械库和仓库领到所需的卡宾枪、马鞍和军服。随后我也南下圣安东尼奥，发现新墨西哥、亚利桑那和俄克拉何马来的人已经到齐，印第安领地来的人在我到达后不久也赶到了。

正是这些人构成了全团的主体，赋予了它独特的气质。他们来自仍留在合众国版图之内的那四个领地；也就是说，来自最近才被白人文明收入囊中的土地——那里的生活条件，最接近尚有边疆时代的边疆。这批西南部人是一等一的人物——身材高大、筋骨结实，久经风霜的脸上一派坚毅，一双眼睛直视着你，毫不畏缩。他们中间各行各业的人都有，但最典型的三种人是：牛仔、猎人，以及采矿探矿人——那种四处漂泊、猎兽为生、把一辈子花在寻矿求富上的人。

===Original===
In all the world there could be no better material for soldiers than that afforded by these grim hunters of the mountains, these wild rough riders of the plains. They were accustomed to handling wild and savage horses; they were accustomed to following the chase with the rifle, both for sport and as a means of livelihood. Varied though their occupations had been, almost all had, at one time or another, herded cattle and hunted big game. They were hardened to life in the open, and to shifting for themselves under adverse circumstances. They were used, for all their lawless freedom, to the rough discipline of the roundup and the mining company. Some of them came from the small frontier towns; but most were from the wilderness, having left their lonely hunters’ cabins and shifting cow-camps to seek new and more stirring adventures beyond the sea.

They had their natural leaders﻿—the men who had shown they could master other men, and could more than hold their own in the eager driving life of the new settlements.

The Captains and Lieutenants were sometimes men who had campaigned in the regular army against Apache, Ute, and Cheyenne, and who, on completing their term of service, had shown their energy by settling in the new communities and growing up to be men of mark. In other cases they were sheriffs, marshals, deputy-sheriffs, and deputy-marshals﻿—men who had fought Indians, and still more often had waged relentless war upon the bands of white desperadoes. There was Bucky O’Neill, of Arizona, Captain of Troop A, the Mayor of Prescott, a famous sheriff throughout the West for his feats of victorious warfare against the Apache, no less than against the white road-agents and man-killers. His father had fought in Meagher’s Brigade in the Civil War; and he was himself a born soldier, a born leader of men. He was a wild, reckless fellow, soft spoken, and of dauntless courage and boundless ambition; he was staunchly loyal to his friends, and cared for his men in every way. There was Captain Llewellen, of New Mexico, a good citizen, a political leader, and one of the most noted peace-officers of the country; he had been shot four times in pitched fights with red marauders and white outlaws. There was Lieutenant Ballard, who had broken up the Black Jack gang of ill-omened notoriety, and his Captain, Curry, another New Mexican sheriff of fame. The officers from the Indian Territory had almost all served as marshals and deputy-marshals; and in the Indian Territory, service as a deputy-marshal meant capacity to fight stand-up battles with the gangs of outlaws.

===Chinese===
普天之下，再也找不出比这些冷峻的山地猎人、这些大平原上的荒野骑手更好的兵源了。他们惯于驾驭狂野凶悍的烈马；惯于背枪行猎，既为消遣，也为谋生。他们的行当虽然五花八门，但几乎人人都放过牛、猎过大兽。他们过惯了露天生涯，惯于在逆境中自谋生计。别看他们自由散漫，却早已习惯赶拢畜群（roundup）和采矿营地那种粗砺的纪律。他们中有少数人来自边疆小镇，但多数来自荒野深处，离开孤零零的猎人小屋和四处漂泊的牧牛营地，漂洋过海来寻求更新鲜、更刺激的冒险。

他们自有其天生的首领——那些早已证明既能驾驭他人、又能在新拓居地那种热火朝天、你追我赶的生活中稳占上风的人。

那些上尉和中尉里，有的是在正规军中参加过讨伐阿帕奇人、尤特人（Ute）和夏延人（Cheyenne）战役的老兵，服役期满后又在新兴城镇扎下根来、渐成头面人物，显出了他们的干才。另有的是县治安官（sheriff）、联邦执法官（marshal）、副县治安官和副执法官——这些人同印第安人交过手，而更多的时候，是对白人匪帮进行过无情的清剿。亚利桑那的巴基·奥尼尔（Bucky O'Neill）就是其中一个：A 连连长、普雷斯科特市市长，在整个西部以治安官闻名——无论是征讨阿帕奇人，还是缉捕白人劫路匪与杀人凶手，他都战功累累。他父亲在南北战争里打过米格旅（Meagher's Brigade）；他自己则是天生的军人、天生的首领。他是个狂放不羁的角色，说起话来轻声细语，胆略无惧，野心无边；对朋友赤胆忠心，对部下无微不至。新墨西哥的卢埃伦上尉（Captain Llewellen）也在其中：一位好公民、政治领袖，全国最负盛名的治安官员之一；在同红种劫掠者和白人匪帮的正面激战里，他挨过四枪。还有巴拉德中尉（Lieutenant Ballard），曾剿灭恶名远扬的「黑杰克」帮；他的连长柯里上尉（Curry），也是新墨西哥一位赫赫有名的治安官。从印第安领地来的军官几乎都当过执法官或副执法官；而在印第安领地，做过副执法官，就意味着有能力同匪帮拉开阵势打硬仗。

===Original===
Three of our higher officers had been in the regular army. One was Major Alexander Brodie, from Arizona, afterward Lieutenant-Colonel, who had lived for twenty years in the Territory, and had become a thorough Westerner without sinking the West Pointer﻿—a soldier by taste as well as training, whose men worshipped him and would follow him anywhere, as they would Bucky O’Neill or any other of their favorites. Brodie was running a big mining business; but when the *Maine* was blown up, he abandoned everything and telegraphed right and left to bid his friends get ready for the fight he saw impending.

Then there was Micah Jenkins, the captain of Troop K, a gentle and courteous South Carolinian, on whom danger acted like wine. In action he was a perfect gamecock, and he won his majority for gallantry in battle.

===Chinese===
我们团有三位高级军官出身正规军。一位是亚利桑那来的亚历山大·布罗迪少校（Major Alexander Brodie），后来升任中校。他在领地生活了二十年，成了地道的西部人，西点军校（West Point）的底子却半点没有丢——论天性、论训练都是军人，士兵们敬爱他，愿意跟他到天涯海角，就像愿意跟着巴基·奥尼尔或他们偏爱的任何其他人一样。布罗迪原本经营着一桩大矿业生意；「缅因」号一炸，他撇下一切，四面八方拍电报，招呼朋友们为他预见的那场恶战早作准备。

还有 K 连连长迈卡·詹金斯（Micah Jenkins），一位温文有礼的南卡罗来纳人，危险之于他，犹如美酒。一到战斗中，他就是一只十足的斗鸡；他的少校军衔，正是凭战场上的英勇挣来的。

===Original===
Finally, there was Allyn Capron, who was, on the whole, the best soldier in the regiment. In fact, I think he was the ideal of what an American regular army officer should be. He was the fifth in descent from father to son who had served in the army of the United States, and in body and mind alike he was fitted to play his part to perfection. Tall and lithe, a remarkable boxer and walker, a first-class rider and shot, with yellow hair and piercing blue eyes, he looked what he was, the archetype of the fighting man. He had under him one of the two companies from the Indian Territory; and he so soon impressed himself upon the wild spirit of his followers, that he got them ahead in discipline faster than any other troop in the regiment, while at the same time taking care of their bodily wants. His ceaseless effort was so to train them, care for them, and inspire them as to bring their fighting efficiency to the highest possible pitch. He required instant obedience, and tolerated not the slightest evasion of duty; but his mastery of his art was so thorough and his performance of his own duty so rigid that he won at once not merely their admiration, but that soldierly affection so readily given by the man in the ranks to the superior who cares for his men and leads them fearlessly in battle.

All﻿—Easterners and Westerners, Northerners and Southerners, officers and men, cowboys and college graduates, wherever they came from, and whatever their social position﻿—possessed in common the traits of hardihood and a thirst for adventure. They were to a man born adventurers, in the old sense of the word.

===Chinese===
最后是阿林·卡普伦（Allyn Capron），总的来说，他是全团最好的军人。事实上，我认为他就是美国正规军军官的理想典范。他是自家第五代父子相承的合众国军人，身心两方面的禀赋，都足以把他的角色扮演到尽善尽美。他身长腰韧，拳击和步行的功夫了得，骑术枪法俱是一流，一头黄发，一双锐利的蓝眼睛，看上去就名副其实，是斗士的原型。他麾下是印第安领地来的两个连中的一个；他那么快就镇住了部下们的野性，使这个连的纪律进步冠于全团，同时又把他们的衣食冷暖照顾得妥妥帖帖。他孜孜以求的，就是训练他们、照料他们、激励他们，把他们的战斗力推到可能的顶点。他要求绝对服从，对丝毫逃避职责的行为绝不容忍；但他对本行之精通、自身履职之严格，使他不仅立刻赢得了他们的钦佩，还赢得了那种士兵们乐于献给关怀部下、临阵身先士卒的长官的军人式的爱戴。

所有人——东部人和西部人、北方人和南方人、军官和士兵、牛仔和大学毕业生，无论来自何方，也无论社会地位如何——都共有吃苦耐劳的品格和对冒险的渴望。他们是清一色天生的冒险家，用的是这个词旧日的含义。

===Original===
The men in the ranks were mostly young; yet some were past their first youth. These had taken part in the killing of the great buffalo herds, and had fought Indians when the tribes were still on the warpath. The younger ones, too, had led rough lives; and the lines in their faces told of many a hardship endured, and many a danger silently faced with grim, unconscious philosophy. Some were originally from the East, and had seen strange adventures in different kinds of life, from sailing round the Horn to mining in Alaska. Others had been born and bred in the West, and had never seen a larger town than Santa Fe or a bigger body of water than the Pecos in flood. Some of them went by their own name; some had changed their names; and yet others possessed but half a name, colored by some adjective, like Cherokee Bill, Happy Jack of Arizona, Smoky Moore, the broncobuster, so named because cowboys often call vicious horses “smoky” horses, and Rattlesnake Pete, who had lived among the Moquis and taken part in the snake-dances. Some were professional gamblers, and, on the other hand, no less than four were or had been Baptist or Methodist clergymen﻿—and proved first-class fighters, too, by the way. Some were men whose lives in the past had not been free from the taint of those fierce kinds of crime into which the lawless spirits who dwell on the borderland between civilization and savagery so readily drift. A far larger number had served at different times in those bodies of armed men with which the growing civilization of the border finally puts down its savagery.

There was one characteristic and distinctive contingent which could have appeared only in such a regiment as ours. From the Indian Territory there came a number of Indians﻿—Cherokees, Chickasaws, Choctaws, and Creeks. Only a few were of pure blood. The others shaded off until they were absolutely indistinguishable from their white comrades; with whom, it may be mentioned, they all lived on terms of complete equality.

===Chinese===
行伍间多为年轻人，但也有一些人早已过了青春年少。这些人大都参加过猎杀大野牛群的行动，在各部落还在四处征战时同印第安人打过仗。年轻些的也过惯了粗粝的生活；他们脸上的皱纹，写满了忍受过的种种磨难，和一次次以冷峻而不自知的达观默默面对过的危险。有些人原籍东部，在各式各样的生涯中见识过奇险——从绕行合恩角的航海到阿拉斯加的采矿。另一些人生于西部、长于西部，见过的最大城镇不过圣菲，见过的最大水面不过是涨水时的佩科斯河。有些人用着本名；有些人改过名字；还有些人只剩半个名字，前面缀着个修饰词，比如切罗基·比尔（Cherokee Bill）、亚利桑那的「快活杰克」（Happy Jack）、驯烈马师「斯莫基」摩尔（Smoky Moore）——之所以叫斯莫基，是因为牛仔们常把凶悍的烈马唤作「冒烟」（smoky）马——还有在莫基人（Moquis，即霍皮人）中间生活过、参加过蛇舞的「响尾蛇皮特」（Rattlesnake Pete）。有些人是职业赌徒；而另一方面，足足有四个当过或者仍是浸信会或卫理公会的牧师——顺便说一句，事实证明也都是一流的战士。有些人过去的行迹，沾染着那些凶悍罪行的污点——栖身于文明与野蛮交界处的亡命之徒，最容易滑入这类罪行。而多得多的人，则先后在各支武装队伍里服过役——日益扩展的边疆文明，最终正是靠这些武装来荡平蛮荒。

有一支颇具特色、别处绝无仅有的队伍，只可能出现在我们这样的团里。从印第安领地来了一批印第安人——切罗基人（Cherokees）、契卡索人（Chickasaws）、乔克托人（Choctaws）和克里克人（Creeks）。纯血统的只有少数。其余的血统渐次稀释，直到与白人战友毫无二致、无从分辨；这里不妨一提，他们同白人战友相处，完全平等。

===Original===
Not all of the Indians were from the Indian Territory. One of the gamest fighters and best soldiers in the regiment was Pollock, a full-blooded Pawnee. He had been educated, like most of the other Indians, at one of those admirable Indian schools which have added so much to the total of the small credit account with which the White race balances the very unpleasant debit account of its dealings with the Red. Pollock was a silent, solitary fellow﻿—an excellent penman, much given to drawing pictures. When we got down to Santiago he developed into the regimental clerk. I never suspected him of having a sense of humor until one day, at the end of our stay in Cuba, as he was sitting in the Adjutant’s tent working over the returns, there turned up a trooper of the First who had been acting as barber. Eyeing him with immovable face Pollock asked, in a guttural voice: “Do you cut hair?” The man answered “Yes”; and Pollock continued, “Then you’d better cut mine,” muttering, in an explanatory soliloquy: “Don’t want to wear my hair long like a wild Indian when I’m in civilized warfare.”

Another Indian came from Texas. He was a brakeman on the Southern Pacific, and wrote telling me he was an American Indian, and that he wanted to enlist. His name was Colbert, which at once attracted my attention; for I was familiar with the history of the Cherokees and Chickasaws during the eighteenth century, when they lived east of the Mississippi. Early in that century various traders, chiefly Scotchmen, settled among them, and the half-breed descendants of one named Colbert became the most noted chiefs of the Chickasaws. I summoned the applicant before me, and found that he was an excellent man, and, as I had supposed, a descendant of the old Chickasaw chiefs.

===Chinese===
印第安人并非全都来自印第安领地。全团最有斗劲的战士、最出色的士兵之一是波洛克（Pollock），一个纯血统的波尼人（Pawnee）。他同多数印第安同伴一样，毕业于那类令人赞叹的印第安学校——白种人同红种人打交道的账簿上有一笔极不体面的亏空，这类学校为冲抵亏空的小小进项栏，增添了不少分量。波洛克沉默寡言、独来独往——写得一手好字，又极爱画画。我们到了圣地亚哥以后，他成了团部书记。我一直不觉得他有什么幽默感，直到我们居留古巴的后期：有一天，他正坐在副官帐篷里理报表，来了个一直兼生理发师的团里士兵。波洛克面无表情地打量他，用粗哑的嗓音问：「你剪头发吗？」那人答「剪」；波洛克接着说：「那你最好把我的剪了。」又自言自语地补了一句，算是解释：「打文明战争的时候，我可不想留一头野印第安人的长发。」

另一个印第安人来自得克萨斯。他是南太平洋铁路上的制动员，写信告诉我说他是美国印第安人，想要入伍。他姓科尔伯特（Colbert），这立刻引起了我的注意；因为十八世纪切罗基人和契卡索人还住在密西西比河以东时的一段历史，我相当熟悉。那个世纪早期，各路商人——主要是苏格兰人——在他们中间定居下来，其中一个姓科尔伯特的，他的混血后代成了契卡索人最有名的首领。我把这位申请人召来当面一谈，发现他是个人品极好的人，而且正如我猜测的那样，是老一辈契卡索首领的后裔。

===Original===
He brought into the regiment, by the way, his “partner,” a white man. The two had been inseparable companions for some years, and continued so in the regiment. Every man who has lived in the West knows that, vindictive though the hatred between the white man and the Indian is when they stand against one another in what may be called their tribal relations, yet that men of Indian blood, when adopted into white communities, are usually treated precisely like anyone else.

Colbert was not the only Indian whose name I recognized. There was a Cherokee named Adair, who, upon inquiry, I found to be descended from the man who, a century and a half ago, wrote a ponderous folio, to this day of great interest, about the Cherokees, with whom he had spent the best years of his life as a trader and agent.

I don’t know that I ever came across a man with a really sweeter nature than another Cherokee named Holderman. He was an excellent soldier, and for a long time acted as cook for the headquarters mess. He was a half-breed, and came of a soldier stock on both sides and through both races. He explained to me once why he had come to the war; that it was because his people always had fought when there was a war, and he could not feel happy to stay at home when the flag was going into battle.

===Chinese===
